\newcommand{\Cx}{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
\newcommand{\wt}{\widetilde{w}}
\newcommand{\Gc}{G_c}

\title{The toral--unipotent factorization $G(\mathfrak{m}) = HU$: a machine-checked proof}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\begin{abstract}
We present a formal verification of Theorem 3.6 of the source (\texttt{thm: gsimple g m eq h u}). The theorem concerns the group $G(\mathfrak{m})$ given by generators and relations attached to the coefficients of the modular function $J$. It states that $G(\mathfrak{m})=HU$, where $H$ is the toral subgroup and $U$ is the unipotent subgroup. The formalization takes the coefficient function as an arbitrary parameter $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and needs no hypothesis on it. The formal proof follows the source argument: it shows that the set $HU$ is a subgroup containing every generator. Closure under products and inverses rests on the previously verified fact that $H$ normalizes $U$ (Theorem 3.4 and Lemma 3.5 of the source).
\end{abstract}

\tableofcontents

\section{Statement}

Let $c(j)$ denote the coefficient of $q^j$ in the modular function
\[
J(q)=\sum_{j\ge -1}c(j)q^j=\frac1q+196884\,q+21493760\,q^2+864299970\,q^3+\cdots,
\]
so that $c(-1)=1$, $c(0)=0$, $c(1)=196884$, and so on.

Define the index set
\[
E=\{(\ell,j,k)\mid j\in\mathbb{N},\ 1\le k\le c(j),\ 0\le \ell<j\}
\]
and, for $0\le\ell<j$, the number
\[
c_{\ell j}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell).
\]

Let $\mathcal{X}$ be the set of symbols
\[
H_1(s),\ H_2(s),\ X_{-1}(u),\ Y_{-1}(u),\ X_{\ell,jk}(u),\ Y_{\ell,jk}(u)
\qquad (s\in\Cx,\ u\in\mathbb{C},\ (\ell,j,k)\in E).
\]
Let $F(\mathcal{X})$ be the free group on $\mathcal{X}$. The symbols $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ are called the \emph{unipotent generators}. The commutator is $(a,b)=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.

For $s,t\in\Cx$, $u,v\in\mathbb{C}$ and $(\ell,j,k),(m,p,q)\in E$, put
\begin{align*}
\tag{Re:0} \wt_{-1}(s)&:=X_{-1}(s)\,Y_{-1}(-s^{-1})\,X_{-1}(s), & \wt_{-1}&:=\wt_{-1}(1),\\
\tag{Im:0} \wt_{\ell,jk}(s)&:=X_{\ell,jk}(s)\,Y_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-s^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr)\,X_{\ell,jk}(s), & \wt_{\ell,jk}&:=\wt_{\ell,jk}(1).
\end{align*}
The set $\mathcal{R}\subseteq F(\mathcal{X})$ consists of the following relations.

\begingroup\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
\tag{H:1a} H_1(s)H_1(t)&=H_1(st),\\
\tag{H:1b} H_2(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(st),\\
\tag{H:2} H_1(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(t)H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:1a} X_{-1}(u)X_{-1}(v)&=X_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:1b} Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v)&=Y_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:2} Y_{-1}(-t)X_{-1}(s)Y_{-1}(t)&=X_{-1}(-t^{-1})\,Y_{-1}(-t^2s)\,X_{-1}(t^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:3} \wt_{-1}(s)\wt_{-1}&=H_1(-s)H_2(-s^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:4a} \wt_{-1}X_{-1}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:4b} \wt_{-1}Y_{-1}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:5a} \wt_{-1}H_1(s)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=H_2(s),\\
\tag{Re:5b} \wt_{-1}H_2(s)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:6a} H_1(s)X_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Re:6b} H_2(s)X_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6c} H_1(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6d} H_2(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Im:1a} X_{\ell,jk}(u)X_{\ell,jk}(v)&=X_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:1b} Y_{\ell,jk}(u)Y_{\ell,jk}(v)&=Y_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:2} Y_{\ell,jk}(-t)X_{\ell,jk}(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(t)&=X_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr)Y_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}t^2s)X_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr),\\
\tag{Im:3} \wt_{\ell,jk}\bigl(s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}\bigr)\wt_{\ell,jk}&=H_1\bigl((-s)^{j-\ell}\bigr)H_2\bigl((-s)^{\ell+1}\bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4a} \wt_{\ell,jk}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(-u/c_{\ell j}),\\
\tag{Im:4b} \wt_{\ell,jk}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}u),\\
\tag{Im:5a} \wt_{\ell,jk}H_1(s^{j-\ell})\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)}),\\
\tag{Im:5b} \wt_{\ell,jk}H_2(s^{\ell+1})\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_1(s^{-(j-\ell)}),\\
\tag{Im:6a} H_1(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{\ell+1}u),\\
\tag{Im:6b} H_2(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}u),\\
\tag{Im:6c} H_1(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(\ell+1)}u),\\
\tag{Im:6d} H_2(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(j-\ell)}u),\\
\tag{U:1a} (X_{-1}(u),X_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1b} (Y_{-1}(u),Y_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1c} (Y_{-1}(u),X_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1d} (X_{-1}(u),Y_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:2} (X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{m,pq}(v))&=1\quad\text{if } j\ne p \text{ or } k\ne q \text{ or } |\ell-m|>1,\\
\tag{U:3a} (X_{1,2k}(u),Y_{0,2k}(v))&=X_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:3b} (X_{0,2k}(u),Y_{1,2k}(v))&=Y_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:4a} \wt_{-1}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr),\\
\tag{U:4b} \wt_{-1}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr).
\end{align*}
\endgroup

The group is
\[
G(\mathfrak{m})=\langle\mathcal{X}\mid\mathcal{R}\rangle=F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}},
\]
where $N_{\mathcal{R}}$ is the normal closure of $\mathcal{R}$. The \emph{toral subgroup} $H$ is the subgroup of $G(\mathfrak{m})$ generated by $\{H_1(s),H_2(s)\mid s\in\Cx\}$. The \emph{unipotent subgroup} $U$ is the subgroup generated by all unipotent generators:
\[
U=\langle X_{-1}(u),Y_{-1}(u),X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\mid u\in\mathbb{C},\ (\ell,j,k)\in E\rangle.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 3.6 of the source]
$G(\mathfrak{m})=HU$.
\end{theorem}

\section{Formalization}

\paragraph{The coefficient function.}
The formalization does not define the coefficients of $J$. It takes an arbitrary function $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ as a parameter, as the problem set allows. All objects below depend on $c$. The source's group is obtained by taking $c(j)$ to be the $q^j$-coefficient of $J$ for $j\in\mathbb{N}$; these are non-negative integers, with $c(0)=0$. The coefficient $c(-1)$ plays no role, since $E$ involves only $j\in\mathbb{N}$.

The present theorem needs no hypothesis on $c$. In particular, the hypothesis $c(2)\ge1$ used elsewhere in the library is not needed here.

\paragraph{The library.}
The following definitions and results come from earlier verified work. The definitions are stated here in mathematical terms.

\begin{definition}[Index set]
For $\ell,j,k\in\mathbb{N}$, the predicate $\mathrm{Valid}_c(\ell,j,k)$ means
\[
\ell<j \quad\text{and}\quad 1\le k\le c(j),
\]
that is, $(\ell,j,k)\in E$.
\end{definition}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Valid} --- library, verified earlier}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Index set E: (ℓ,j,k) with 0 ≤ ℓ \PYZlt{} j and 1 ≤ k ≤ c(j). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Valid}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\begin{definition}[Generating symbols]
The type $\mathrm{Gen}_c$ of symbols has six kinds of elements:
\begin{itemize}
\item $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\Cx$;
\item $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb{C}$;
\item $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for $\ell,j,k\in\mathbb{N}$, a proof of $\mathrm{Valid}_c(\ell,j,k)$, and $u\in\mathbb{C}$.
\end{itemize}
Proofs of a proposition are all equal in Lean, so this is the set $\mathcal{X}$ of the source.

The free group $F(\mathcal{X})$ is $F_c$. The elements $h_1(s)$, $h_2(s)$, $x_{-1}(u)$, $y_{-1}(u)$, $x_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ of $F_c$ are the corresponding basis elements.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Auxiliary elements]
The library defines the following elements.
\begin{itemize}
\item The complex number
\[
c_{\ell j}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j\mathbin{\dot-}\ell),
\]
where $\dot-$ is truncated subtraction on $\mathbb{N}$. It equals $j-\ell$ whenever $\ell\le j$.
\item The commutator $\mathrm{cm}(a,b)=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.
\item The relator $\mathrm{eqr}(a,b)=ab^{-1}$, which encodes the equation $a=b$.
\item The words $\wt_{-1}(s)$ and $\wt_{\ell,jk}(s)$ for $s\in\Cx$, defined exactly as in (Re:0) and (Im:0).
\end{itemize}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Relators and the group]
The predicate $\mathrm{Rel}_c$ on $F_c$ has one constructor for each relation of the source. The relator is $ab^{-1}$ for a relation $a=b$, and $(a,b)$ itself for a relation $(a,b)=1$. Its parameters range over the same sets as in the source: $s,t\in\Cx$, $u,v\in\mathbb{C}$, and triples in $E$. A few points of the encoding should be noted.
\begin{itemize}
\item In (Im:5a,b) and (Im:6c,d), the negative powers $s^{-n}$ are written $(s^n)^{-1}$.
\item (U:1a,b) are indexed by $j,k$ with $(j-1,j,k)\in E$. (U:1c,d) are indexed by $j,k$ with $(0,j,k)\in E$. (U:3a,b) are indexed by $k$ with $(0,2,k),(1,2,k)\in E$.
\item (U:4a,b) carry the additional hypothesis $(j-1-\ell,j,k)\in E$. This follows from $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, so the relations imposed are exactly those of the source. All subtractions there are truncated, but they are exact because $\ell<j$.
\end{itemize}
The set $\mathrm{Rels}_c$ collects these relators. The group $\Gc=\mathrm{Grp}_c$ is the presented group $F_c/N$, where $N$ is the normal closure of $\mathrm{Rels}_c$.

We write $\pi\colon F_c\to\Gc$ for the quotient map, and use the same letters for generators and their images in $\Gc$.
\end{definition}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Rel} --- library, verified earlier}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]

\begin{definition}[Toral and unipotent subgroups]
The set $\mathrm{torusGens}_c\subseteq\Gc$ consists of the elements $\pi(h_1(s))$ and $\pi(h_2(s))$ for $s\in\Cx$. The subgroup $H=\mathrm{torusSub}_c$ is the subgroup it generates.

The set $\mathrm{unipGens}_c$ consists of the images of
\[
x_{-1}(u),\quad y_{-1}(u),\quad x_{\ell,jk}(u),\quad y_{\ell,jk}(u)\qquad (u\in\mathbb{C},\ (\ell,j,k)\in E).
\]
The subgroup $U=\mathrm{unipSub}_c$ is the subgroup it generates.

These are precisely the subgroups $H$ and $U$ of the source.
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Library results used in this proof.}
The library contains many verified results about $\Gc$, for instance the perfectness of $\Gc$ (Theorem 3.3 of the source, \leanname{gsimple_g_m_perfect}). Only the following are used here.

\leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens}: for every $c$, every subgroup $N\le\Gc$ that contains the image of every symbol in $\mathrm{Gen}_c$ equals $\Gc$. This holds because the images of the symbols generate the presented group.

\leanname{gsimple_torus_unipotent_commute} (Lemma 3.5 of the source): for every $c$, every $h\in H$ and every $u\in U$, there is $u'\in U$ with $uh=hu'$. It is proved with $u'=h^{-1}uh$, using the previous result.

\paragraph{The target statement.}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: target statement \texttt{gsimple\_g\_m\_eq\_h\_u}, fixed before the proof; \texttt{solution} is checked to have exactly this type}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{gsimple\PYZus{}g\PYZus{}m\PYZus{}eq\PYZus{}h\PYZus{}u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Grp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{torusSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

The target statement, proved below as \leanname{solution}, reads as follows. For every $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and every $g\in\Gc$, there exist $h\in H$ and $u\in U$ with $g=hu$.

This is the inclusion $\Gc\subseteq HU$. The reverse inclusion $HU\subseteq\Gc$ holds trivially in the formalization, because products of elements of $\Gc$ lie in $\Gc$ by typing. The source's equality $G(\mathfrak{m})=HU$ is therefore exactly what is proved.

The function $c$ stands for the coefficients of $J$. Since no hypothesis on $c$ is needed, the theorem holds in particular for the source's choice of $c$.

\section{Proof}

\subsection{Overview}
Write $HU=\{hu\mid h\in H,\ u\in U\}\subseteq\Gc$. The source proof has three steps, and the formal proof follows them:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $HU$ is closed under multiplication (\cref{lem:hu_mul}), by Lemma 3.5 of the source.
\item $HU$ is closed under inversion (\cref{lem:hu_inv}), again by Lemma 3.5.
\item $HU$ contains every generator (\cref{lem:hu_gen}).
\end{enumerate}
The formal proof makes one point explicit that the source leaves implicit: $1\in HU$ (\cref{lem:hu_one}). This is needed to obtain a subgroup.

The last step uses the library result \leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens}: a subgroup containing all generators is the whole group. Lemma 3.5 is the library result \leanname{gsimple_torus_unipotent_commute}.

\Cref{lem:hu_of_torus,lem:hu_of_unip} record the inclusions $H\subseteq HU$ and $U\subseteq HU$. They were proved as auxiliaries but are not used in the final argument: \cref{lem:hu_gen} handles the generators directly.

There is no mathematical deviation from the source proof.

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{hu_mul}]\label{lem:hu_mul}
Let $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and $a,b\in\Gc$. Suppose $a=h_1u_1$ and $b=h_2u_2$ with $h_1,h_2\in H$ and $u_1,u_2\in U$. Then there exist $h\in H$ and $u\in U$ with $ab=hu$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply \leanname{gsimple_torus_unipotent_commute} to $h_2\in H$ and $u_1\in U$. This gives $u_1'\in U$ with $u_1h_2=h_2u_1'$. Then
\[
ab=h_1u_1h_2u_2=h_1(u_1h_2)u_2=h_1(h_2u_1')u_2=(h_1h_2)(u_1'u_2).
\]
Here $h_1h_2\in H$ and $u_1'u_2\in U$, since $H$ and $U$ are subgroups.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{hu_inv}]\label{lem:hu_inv}
Let $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and $a\in\Gc$ with $a=hu$ for some $h\in H$ and $u\in U$. Then there exist $h'\in H$ and $u'\in U$ with $a^{-1}=h'u'$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $H$ and $U$ are subgroups, $h^{-1}\in H$ and $u^{-1}\in U$. Apply \leanname{gsimple_torus_unipotent_commute} to $h^{-1}$ and $u^{-1}$. This gives $u'\in U$ with $u^{-1}h^{-1}=h^{-1}u'$. Hence
\[
a^{-1}=(hu)^{-1}=u^{-1}h^{-1}=h^{-1}u',
\]
and we take $h'=h^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{hu_one}]\label{lem:hu_one}
For every $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, there exist $h\in H$ and $u\in U$ with $1=hu$ in $\Gc$.
\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{rfl}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
We argue by cases on the kind of symbol.
\begin{itemize}
\item If $g=H_1(s)$ or $g=H_2(s)$ with $s\in\Cx$, its image lies in $\mathrm{torusGens}_c\subseteq H$. Take $h$ to be this image and $u=1$.
\item If $g$ is one of $X_{-1}(u_0)$, $Y_{-1}(u_0)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u_0)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u_0)$, its image lies in $\mathrm{unipGens}_c\subseteq U$. Take $h=1$ and $u$ to be this image.
\end{itemize}
In each case the required equation holds by definition.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Proof of the theorem}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{solution} --- main proof from the captain's sketch}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{solution}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hg}

\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Fix $c$ and let $S=HU=\{x\in\Gc\mid \exists\,h\in H,\ u\in U:\ x=hu\}$. By \cref{lem:hu_mul}, \cref{lem:hu_one} and \cref{lem:hu_inv}, $S$ is closed under multiplication, contains $1$, and is closed under inversion. So $S$ is the carrier of a subgroup $N\le\Gc$.

By \cref{lem:hu_gen}, $N$ contains the image of every generating symbol. The library result \leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens} then gives $N=\Gc$.

Hence every $g\in\Gc$ lies in $S$, that is, $g=hu$ for some $h\in H$ and $u\in U$. This proves $\Gc=HU$.
\end{proof}

\section{Independent review}

A referee model (claude-opus-5-5), which took no part in the formalization or the proof, compared the verified Lean file with the source. Verdict: \textbf{FAITHFUL}.

\paragraph{Definitions.} \begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{GM.Valid} encodes the index set $E$: the condition $\ell<j$, $1\le k\le c(j)$. Here $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ is a parameter, which the formalization note permits. Since $\ell<j$ is required, $j\ge 1$ automatically.
\item \leanname{GM.Gen} encodes the symbol set $\mathcal{X}$: $H_1(s)$, $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\mathbb{C}^\times$; $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb{C}$; and $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for valid triples. \leanname{GM.F} is the free group on it.
\item \leanname{GM.cc} matches $c_{\ell j}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)$. The natural-number subtraction $j-\ell$ is harmless because $\ell<j$.
\item \leanname{GM.wm} and \leanname{GM.ww} match (Re:0) and (Im:0).
\item \leanname{GM.cm} is $aba^{-1}b^{-1}$. \leanname{GM.eqr} $a\,b^{-1}$ is the relator for $a=b$.
\item \leanname{GM.Rel}: I compared every constructor with the source.
  \begin{itemize}
  \item (H:1a), (H:1b), (H:2), (Re:1a)--(Re:6d) and (Im:1a)--(Im:6d) agree with the source. This includes the inverted exponents in (Im:5a), (Im:5b), (Im:6c), (Im:6d) and the $c_{\ell j}$ factors in (Im:2) and (Im:4).
  \item (U:1a)--(U:1d) are stated with validity witnesses. For $j=0$, the side condition $\mathrm{Valid}(j-1,j,k)$ is false, which matches the source because there is no such element of $E$.
  \item (U:2) uses the disjunction $j\ne p\lor k\ne q\lor |\ell-m|>1$, as in the source.
  \item (U:3a), (U:3b), (U:4a), (U:4b) agree with the source. In (U:4) the extra witness for $(j-1-\ell,j,k)$ is always available when $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, so no instance is lost.
  \end{itemize}
\item \leanname{GM.Grp} is the presented group $F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$.
\item \leanname{GM.torusSub} is the closure of the images of $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$, i.e. $H$.
\item \leanname{GM.unipSub} is the closure of the images of all unipotent generators, i.e. $U$.
\end{itemize}
No discrepancies found. The relation set is not degenerate.

\paragraph{Statement.} The target is: for every $c$ and every $g\in G(\mathfrak m)$ there exist $h\in H$ and $u\in U$ with $g=hu$. This says exactly $G(\mathfrak m)\subseteq HU$; the reverse inclusion is trivial. The source's equality $G(\mathfrak m)=HU$ therefore follows.

No hypothesis on $c$ is assumed, so the statement is more general than the source. The source's statement is recovered by taking $c(j)$ to be the coefficients of $J$ for $j\ge 1$; the value $c(0)$ is irrelevant because $j\ge1$ is forced.

\paragraph{Proof.} The proof follows the source proof directly.
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{hu_mul} gives closure of $HU$ under multiplication via $h_1u_1h_2u_2=(h_1h_2)(u_1'u_2)$.
\item \leanname{hu_inv} gives closure under inversion via $(hu)^{-1}=u^{-1}h^{-1}=h^{-1}u'$.
\item \leanname{hu_one} shows $1\in HU$.
\item These three lemmas package $HU$ as a subgroup $N$.
\item \leanname{hu_gen} shows that every generator lies in $N$.
\item \leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens}, which uses \leanname{PresentedGroup.closure_range_of}, gives $N=\top$.
\end{itemize}

Lemma 3.5 (\leanname{gsimple_torus_unipotent_commute}) is proved in the file, not assumed. It rests on Theorem 3.4 (\leanname{gsimple_torus_normalizes_unipotent}), which is also proved in the file:
\begin{itemize}
\item relations (Re:6) and (Im:6) show that conjugating a unipotent generator by $H_1(s)^{\pm1}$ or $H_2(s)^{\pm1}$ gives a unipotent generator;
\item closure induction then extends this to all of $H$ and $U$;
\item inverses of torus generators are handled by \leanname{h1_inv_mk} and \leanname{h2_inv_mk}, derived from (H:1a) and (Re:5a).
\end{itemize}

The perfectness results from Theorem 3.3 in the file are not used by \leanname{solution}, apart from the auxiliary lemmas \leanname{h2_eq_conj} and \leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens}. Neither of these needs the hypothesis $c(2)\ge1$.

\paragraph{Concerns.} None
